Challenge these proposals under ONLY the supplied task rubric. Evaluate each criterion pass/fail/unknown with score 0–5 and a concrete rationale. Use unknown for evidence gaps, fail for established constraint violations. Entailment concerns reported facts, not proof of an explicitly untested prediction. An unmeasured bottleneck can be the first falsification question; do not demand a proven speedup before proposing a test. No-GPU-execution constraints prohibit launching jobs in this research session, not describing a later bounded experiment. A full-text source existing does not mean the quoted passage supports the claim. No search hits is uncertainty. Novelty is irrelevant unless explicitly enabled by the rubric. Score 0–5 with explicit objections. High polish must not rescue a failed gate. Known techniques, feature activation and ports are legitimate candidates if useful relative to the baseline. An explicit cheap baseline/eligibility check can resolve unknown deployment inclusion before implementation. Do not repeat historical novelty rejection as a current veto. Review each ID exactly once.

"[Bug][Humming] MXFP4 weight + block-FP8 activation is listed as supported on Hopper, but humming-kernels 0.1.16 rejects it\n#60600\nNew issue\nCopy link\nNew issue\nCopy link\nClosed\nClosed\n[Bug][Humming] MXFP4 weight + block-FP8 activation is listed as supported on Hopper, but humming-kernels 0.1.16 rejects it\n#60600\nCopy link\nLabels\nquantization\nDescription\nsimon-veitner-redhat\nopened\non Oct 8, 2026\nIssue body actions\nYour current environment\nvLLM main at\nd5ee309\nhumming-kernels 0.1.16 (the pin in\nrequirements/cuda.txt\n)\n8x H200 (SM90), CUDA 13.0\n\ud83d\udc1b Describe the bug\nHummingExpertsBase._supports_quant_scheme()\nlists MXFP4 weights with block-FP8 activations (group 128, float32 scales) as a supported MoE pair. The comment says it works on Hopper (\nfused_humming_moe.py#L325-L328\n):\n# MXFP4 weight (group-32 e8m0) with block-FP8 activation\n# (group-128 float32). Runs via WGMMA software dequant, so it\n# works on Hopper (SM90/H200) as well as Blackwell.\n(\nkMxfp4Static\n,\nkFp8Dynamic128Sym\n),\ntests/quantization/test_humming_mxfp4_block_fp8.py\n(from\n#51332\n) asserts that this pair is allowed.\nhumming-kernels 0.1.16 refuses the pair.\nis_humming_schema_compatible()\nin\nhumming/schema/humming.py\n(lines 499 and 501) requires group-wise input scales to be e8m0 or e4m3 when the weights are FP4 with group scales:\nif\ninput_group_size\n>\n0\nand\nas_dtype\nnot\nin\n[\ndtypes\n.\nfloat8e8m0\n,\ndtypes\n.\nfloat8e4m3\n]:\nreturn\nFalse\nif\ninput_group_size\n>\n0\nand\nweight_group_size\n>\n0\nand\nas_dtype\n!=\nbs_dtype\n:\nreturn\nFalse\nThe rule does not depend on the GPU. So vLLM's backend selection accepts\nkFp8Dynamic128Sym\nwith MXFP4 weights, and the run then fails at weight load in\ncheck_and_fallback_input_schema()\n. With\nallow_fallback=True\nthe input silently becomes per-token FP8 instead.\nMinimal check on an H200:\nfrom\nvllm\n.\nmodel_executor\n.\nlayers\n.\nquantization\n.\nutils\n.\nhumming\nimport\n(\ncheck_and_fallback_input_schema\n,\nquant_key_to_input_schema\n,\n)\nfrom\nvllm\n.\nmodel_executor\n.\nlayers\n.\nquantization\n.\nutils\n.\nquant_utils\nimport\nkFp8Dynamic128Sym\nfrom\nvllm\n.\nutils\n.\nhumming\nimport\nBaseWeightSchema\nimport\ntorch\ncheck_and_fallback_input_schema\n(\nBaseWeightSchema\n.\nfrom_config\n({\n\"quant_method\"\n:\n\"mxfp4\"\n}),\nquant_key_to_input_schema\n(\nkFp8Dynamic128Sym\n),\nparam_dtype\n=\ntorch\n.\nbfloat16\n,\nallow_fallback\n=\nFalse\n,\n)\n# ValueError: No compatible Humming input schema for HummingInputSchema(...,\n#   input_scale_group_size=128, input_scale_dtype=float32, ...) with weight schema\n#   HummingWeightSchema(b_dtype=float4e2m1, bs_dtype=float8e8m0, weight_scale_group_size=32, ...)\n#   on SM90 (allow_fallback=False)\nThe kernel itself can run this layout. When the compatibility check is bypassed, a dense MXFP4 GEMM with group-128 float32 input scales runs on H200 and its output matches the reference (kernel error 0.0017-0.0032, the same level as per-token FP8). So the mismatch is between vLLM's supported-pairs table and humming's schema rule, not a missing kernel.\nA related limit on SM90: MXFP8 inputs (group 32, e8m0 scales) pass\nis_humming_schema_compatible()\nbut have no SM90 kernel.\nhumming/tune/candidate.py:287\nrejects every WGMMA candidate with \"WGMMA input scales must use float32 storage, got float8e8m0\", and\nhumming/tune/sm90_policies.py:256\nthen raises \"no legal grouped-scale SM90 schedule\".\nFor context, the dense-GEMM probe on H200 (N=2048, K=4096, MXFP4 weights) also showed that group-wise input scales are much slower than per-token on WGMMA at M=4096: 535.7 us for group 128 and 1720 us for group 32 (fp32 scales), against 72.2 us for per-token FP8 and 110.9 us for BF16.\nQuestions\nShould humming accept float32 group scales with MXFP4 weights on SM90 (the kernel runs), or should vLLM drop\n(kMxfp4Static, kFp8Dynamic128Sym)\non SM90 and fix the comment and the test?\nIs e8m0 input-scale support on WGMMA planned? Without it, MXFP8 activations cannot run on Hopper.\nPR\n#59488\nmakes\n--quantization-config.moe.activation\nselect the Humming MoE activation format for MXFP4 checkpoints. Until this is settled, it refuses\nfp8_per_block_dynamic\nand\nmxfp8\non SM90 Humming at startup.\nBefore submitting a new issue...\nMake sure you already searched for relevant issues, and asked the chatbot living at the bottom right corner of the\ndocumentation page\n, which can answer lots of frequently asked questions.\nAI assistance: Claude Code (Anthropic) was used to run the probes and draft this issue. This PR sets\nactivation_key = kFp8Dynamic128Sym\nin that case.\n#59815\nalready plumbs the key into Humming's input schema with fallback disabled. The DeepSeek V4 checkpoint declares block FP8 GS128 with UE8M0 scales for its FP8 layers, so block FP8 matches what the model was built for.\nWhy only with EP: under EP each rank runs full-size experts (hidden 4096, intermediate 2048), where Humming's FP8 x MXFP4 kernels are faster. Under TP4 the intermediate is split to 512 per rank, and dynamic activation quantization costs more than it saves. However, in memory-constrained inference settings, expert weights must be offloaded to CPU, creati\n[... excerpt gap ...]\n\n7 Conclusion\n\n\n\n\nMoEs enable scaling to hundreds of billions of parameters via sparse activations (Fedus et al., 2022). Under memory-constrained settings that rely on offloading, inference becomes I/O-bound due to CPU-GPU expert transfers, reducing TPOT. We introduce an inference-time expert prefetching scheme that speculates future expert selection from internal model representations, enabling expert transfers to overlap with computation and improving TPOT. Our parameter-free approach uses the quasi-hidden state to predict next-layer experts over several modern MoE architectures, while a lightweight estimator selectively improves hit rate over high drift layers. Integrated into the YALIS inference engine, our approach achieves up to 5-14% TPOT improvement over on-demand expert loading while largely preserving downstream task accuracy. By reducing the cost incurred by expert offloading, this work makes large open-source MoE models more practical for local deployment on consumer hardware.\n\n\n\n\n8 Future Work\n\n\n\n\nOur analysis of prefetching focused primarily on CPU-GPU expert offloading settings on consumer and single-GPU datacenter hardware. A natural extension is to study more resource-constrained deployments (e.g., smartphones, ro\n[... excerpt gap ...]\n\n Experimental support, please\n view the build logs\n for errors. "body": "`dispatch(..., borrow_recv=True)` skips the activation copy of the dispatch epilogue: the epilogue writes only metadata plus a row map and returns a `DispatchRecvView` (`slab`, `row_indices`) over the communication buffer, which an indexed consumer such as a gathered GEMM reads directly. The view holds an exclusive lease: `release(*streams)` orders the next communication after the consumers, and dispatch/combine/LB calls/`destroy()` reject an active view. Supported: fresh compact BF16 dispatch within one NVLink domain with CPU counts and `defer_epilogue=False`; everything else is rejected up front. Default behavior is unchanged.\n\n8\u00d7H200, deterministic, hidden 3072, top-8, 16 local experts, dispatch plus the same indexed consumer: \u221213.1% at 8192 tokens/rank and \u221213.5% at 16384, but +15.9% at 1024, hence opt-in. Fixed-route serial forward only; no overlap or training-throughput claim. Combined with DeepGEMM #463 in a live EP8 + gate/up GEMM + SwiGLU step: \u221217%. Raw cycles and the reproducer: [gist](https://gist.github.com/yuweih205/d3fd1e4c3fcb97364f36ec8fda996cfe).\n\nTest: `python tests/ep/test_recv_view.py --num-processes 8` (bitwise payload/metadata against the gathered inputs, replay/combine after release, delayed cross-stream readers, invalid-mode rejection).\n\nRe-validated 2026-10-16 at 71157be (100350f differs only in documentation; native code byte-identical to the measured build) on 8\u00d7H200, PyTorch 2.13.0+cu132: `test_recv_view.py` 16/16 cases under both `EP_AVOID_RECORD_STREAM` policies; the EP8 functional suite passes with CPU counts for direct and deferred epilogues. With `--do-cpu-sync 0` the unmodified suite stops at its full-capacity `recv_x_twice` comparison on this branch exactly as on upstream main; with the valid-prefix comparison from #786 it passes on The internal dispatch copy epilogue still runs. This is not borrowed communication-buffer storage or a zero-copy dispatch implementation.\n\n## Regression coverage\n\nThe new `tests/ep/test_recv_lifetime.py` uses the independent NCCL dispatch reference and requires bitwise equality of payloads, local expert indices, routing weights, source-token metadata, and the saved-activation weight gradient.\n\nEach process run covers:\n\n- Deterministic dispatch off/on; output allocation on the compute/communication stream.\n- Uneven inputs, balanced routes, masked routes, and highly skewed routing with empty receivers.\n- Two selected experts on the same destination rank, distinguishing unique receive rows from token/expert assignments.\n- A prefix saved with `ctx.save_for_backward`, followed by three additional dispatches through the same buffer before backward.\n- A delayed reader on another stream, dropping the original receive allocation references and allocating/filling four same-capacity tensors on the allocation stream. The copy destination and allocator warm-up are prepared before the delay. Pressure writes must finish while a **pre-read** event is still pending; a pending post-copy event alone is insufficient.\n\nScheduling can miss the pressure window. All ranks agree on numerical correctness and window validity, then retry in collective lockstep with a longer delay (at most three attempts). Actual corruption fails immediately; an inconclusive window is never silently reported as coverage.\n\nThe consumer handoff uses a CUDA event captured **after the first dispatch-event wait**, including its deterministic-sort hook. It does not depend on repeated waits on the original `EventOverlap`; that is separate from this lifetime coverage.\n\n## Validation\n\n### Current polished revision\n\nHead: `97fa6d18ff717df99acbdbb862325bda3d4a0d46`, based on public main `b5a33eefda39485f438dc764695b71ba7b9249b6`.\n\nThe current 166-line test passed on H200 with PyTorch `2.13.0+cu132`, CUDA 13.2 and NCCL 2.32.3. Keeping routed-expert weights in MXFP4 enables native reuse of Hugging Face MXFP4 MoE checkpoints, while substantially reducing GPU memory pressure by avoiding a persistent FP8 weight expansion.\nSummary\nUnified SM90 persistent execution.\nUnlike\n[SM90] Add FP8 MegaMoE support\n#383\n, this implementation fuses dispatch, routed/shared L1 and L2, and combine into one persistent kernel. It adapts the SM100 ring-buffer and wave-interleaved scheduler to a cluster-free Hopper design using register-resident WGMMA, one CTA per task, and two resident CTAs per SM.\nHumming-inspired MXFP4\u00d7FP8 compute path.\nRouted weights remain packed MXFP4 in global memory. Model-load preprocessing rebases the K32 UE8M0 exponents into bounded relative scales plus one FP32 secondary scale per expert, following the fused MXFP4\u00d7FP8 approach used by Humming. During execution, packed E2M1 tiles are loaded into compact shared-memory staging, decoded and expanded on the fly into E4M3 FP8 tiles, and then consumed by Hopper WGMMA. This avoids materializing a persistent FP8 copy of the routed weights.\nBenchmark Results\nThe benchmark follows the H20 workload shapes and timing methodology from\n#383\n. When a store tile extends past an expert's capacity, it can overwrite valid rows of the next expert. This affects both BF16 and FP8: capacity 32 reproduces it, and capacity 96 also fails when experts are nearly full.\n\nKeep the expert dimension in the output descriptor and reuse the existing 3D TMA store path. This preserves tile selection and the compute pipeline without adding buffers or synchronization. Boundary regressions are integrated into the existing BF16 and FP8 test suites.\n\nThis revisits the limitation discussed in #15 and the small-capacity failure reported in #268. #15 previously fixed small capacities by shrinking the store tile and rejected larger capacities not divisible by the tile size; it also suggested 3D TMA but deferred support for those cases. This change handles both small and non-tile-aligned capacities through the same output path.\n\nOn H200, the median paired latency changes across six rounds were between \u22120.17% and +0.16% for the larger tested shapes. The smallest shape was about 1.9% slower for BF16 and 1.5% slower for FP8, roughly 0.06\u20130.08 \u00b5s. The uniform 3D path avoids maintaining separate aligned and unaligned implementations, at that small measured cost.\n\n<details>\n<summary>Reproduction and validation</summary>\n\nRequires an SM90 GPU and a source build of DeepGEMM. Save the following as `repro.py` outside the source checkout and run `python repro.py` against the installed baseline or patched package:\n\n```python\nimport torch\nimport deep_gemm\nfrom deep_gemm.testing import calc_diff\n\ntorch.manual_seed(29)\na = torch.randn((8, 32, 256), device='cuda', dtype=torch.bfloat16)\nb = torch.randn((8, 256, 256), device='cuda', dtype=torch.bfloat16)\nd = torch.empty((8, 32, 256), device='cuda', dtype=torch.bfloat16)\ncounts = torch.tensor([0, 1, 16, 31, 32, 1, 32, 32],\n                      device='cuda', dtype=torch.int)\nvalid = torch.arange(32, device='cuda')[None, :] < counts[:, None]\nreference = torch.bmm(a.double(), b.double().mT).bfloat16()\n\nfor _ in range(16):\n    deep_gemm.m_grouped_bf16_gemm_nt_masked(a, b, d, counts, 32)\n    diff = calc_diff(d[valid], reference[valid])\n    assert diff < 1e-5, f'{diff=}'\n```\n\nRunning this exact script against the baseline failed with `AssertionError: diff=tensor(0.1456, device='cuda:0', dtype=torch.float64)`; the patched wheel completed all 16 checks. Only valid rows are checked; padding values are unspecified.\n\nRun the included regressions from the checkout after building and installing the patched package:\n\n```bash\ncd tests\npython - <<'PY'\nimport torch\nimport test_bf16\nimport test_fp8_fp4\n\ntorch.manual_seed(17)\ntest_bf16.test_m_grouped_gemm_masked_boundaries()\ntest_fp8_fp4.test_m_grouped_gemm_masked_boundaries()\nPY\n```\n\nBoth passed with the final built wheel, including a fresh-JIT-cache run. Both fail at capacity 16 when run against the baseline package. A separate timed run with the JIT cache populated took 0.23 s for the BF16 test body and 0.09 s for FP8; this excludes process startup and imports, and does not estimate cold compilation time.\n\nSupplemental validation, using separate local harnesses not included in this PR:\n\n- Base: `057ca5964aae0879ff2e0eb71ee05a3cb0ba3df7`; candidate: `20865936a058d55c4c583cc2b0c2bcf3bc319a2f`. H200, PyTorch `2.13.0+cu130`, CUDA 13.0.\n- 34 configurations per arm, covering BF16 and FP8, capacities from 1 to 257, single-expert and all-empty cases; 16 eager and 16 Graph calls each. Baseline and revert each had 24 failing configurations; the fix had none. Two configurations were all-empty and only checked completion. The revert reused the original package in a fresh process/cache.\n- Independent FP64 matmul references, rounded to BF16; FP8 references used the actual quantized inputs and scales. No run-to-run changes were observed in the fixed panel.\n- 37 adjacent-path checks passed, covering ordinary GEMM layouts, output dtypes and accumulation, batched GEMM, and K-grouped GEMM.\n- Native `python setup.py bdist_wheel` passed with setuptools `75.8.0`.\n\nPerformance used six paired rounds with alternating arm order. Each measurement took the median of seven CUDA-event samples, each containing 1,000 Graph calls. Input generation and quantization were outside timing; FP8 API scale conversion was included. Positive values below mean slower; values are medians of paired latency changes.\n\n| Groups | Capacity | N | K | Expected M | BF16 | FP8 |\n|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|\n| 8 | 128 | 256 | 256 | 64 | +1.931% | +1.526% |\n| 32 | 4096 | 1536 | 2048 | 20 | +0.070% | \u22120.143% |\n| 6 | 4096 | 4096 | 4096 | 1024 | +0.155% | +0.023% |\n| 32 | 4096 | 6144 | 7168 | 192 | \u22120.167% | \u22120.056% |\n\nClocks were not locked; sampled SM clocks ranged from 1305 to 1980 MHz. link\nCopy Markdown\nCollaborator\n\ud83e\udd16 ds-review-bot Code Review\nv6\nThe inference path now applies the RMSNorm weight fusion consistently with the grad-enabled path, and the added tests cover both weighted and unweighted cases.\nv4\nThe MR fixes bug\n#22\n: the no-grad (inference)\nmhc_pre\npath sent the RAW\nfn\nweight into the fused\nmhc_pre_big_fuse\nkernel (which has no\nnorm_weight\nargument) and therefore SILENTLY DROPPED the optional RMSNorm\nnorm_weight\napplied by the grad-enabled path. The fix folds\nnorm_weight\ninto\nfn\nBEFORE the fused GEMM: since the weight is constant during inference,\n(residual * norm_weight) @ fn == residual @ (fn * norm_weight)\n, and the fused kernel divides by the SAME (unweighted) rms-denominator as the grad path and the torch reference; dtype/round-to-tf32 handling also match, so the math is CORRECT. A regression test is added that compares grad vs no-grad outputs (with/without\nnorm_weight\n) over\ntorch.is_grad_enabled()\n. Main concerns: (1) the test uses an extremely tight\natol/rtol = 1e-6\nbetween two op-fusion/rounding/reduction-order-DIFFERENT kernels plus very large shapes, risking flaky/over-strict failures and slow CI; (2) the fusion logic is DUPLICATED/inlined at a low level across the two paths, which is itself the kind of divergence that caused\n#22\n(the grad path wraps the same\nfn * norm_weight\nmerge in an autograd Function). Cleaning this into one helper selecting the autograd/raw-forward merge on\ntorch.is_grad_enabled()\nwould both remove the duplication and prevent future divergence. Overall: correct in substance, but I would request minor changes to test tolerance/duplication.\nv3\nThis PR fixes a correctness bug in the no-grad (inference) path of\nmhc_pre\nin\ntile_kernels/modeling/mhc/functional.py\n. Previously, when\ntorch.is_grad_enabled()\nwas False, the code called\nmhc_pre_big_fuse(...)\nwithout ever applying\nnorm_weight\n, since the fused kernel does not accept a\nnorm_weight\nargument. As a result the RMSNorm weight was silently dropped in inference mode, diverging from the grad path (which fuses it via\n_MHCFnNormwMerge\ninside\nmhc_pre_norm_fn\n). The fix merges\nnorm_weight\ninto\nfn\nbefore calling the fused kernel using the same\n_mhc_fn_normw_merge_fwd\nkernel used by the grad path, so both paths are now numerically consistent.\nThe fix is correct and minimal: it mirrors the existing grad-path implementation exactly (\ntorch.empty_like(fn)\n+\n_mhc_fn_normw_merge_fwd(*fn.shape)(fn, norm_weight, merged_fn)\n), so inference and training now produce identical results. The new regression test\ntests/mhc/test_functional_mhc_pre.py\nparametrizes over\ngenerate_norm_weight\nin {False, True} and asserts the grad-enabled and no-grad results match for\nlayer_input\n,\npost_mix\n, and\ncomb_mix\n. g^(m / (sqrt(g) + 1)) * sigmoid(g) : g * sigmoid(g)   # negative half is SiLU\r\nout      = min(PowLU(gate), limit) * clamp(up, -limit, limit)            # clamp after the activation\r\n```\r\n\r\n- Adds `ActivationType::PowLU = 11` (`InvalidType` \u2192 12) in all three enum copies, the\r\n  serializer, and **both** `isGatedActivation()` predicates \u2014 the CUTLASS copy in\r\n  `moe_gemm_kernels.h` is the one that drives GEMM1's `N = 2\u00b7inter_size` and workspace sizing.\r\n- Implements `PowLUAdaptor` (fp32, element-wise sign branch, `__powf`), wired into\r\n  `doGatedActivation`'s dispatch and `doActivation`'s enum-indexed `fn_list`, which gains a\r\n  bounds check against out-of-range enum values.\r\n- **No `ActivationParams` ABI change**: `m` rides the generic per-expert `swiglu_alpha` slot\r\n  and the post-activation clamp rides `swiglu_limit` \u2014 the same trade `SwigluStepAdaptor`\r\n  makes for its `limit`. Per-expert limits cover the routed-vs-fused-shared-expert split.\r\n- Python: typed `PowLU(m=2.5, limit=None)` activation value, CUTLASS lowering\r\n  (default `m` needs no tensor; `gemm1_beta` has no PowLU meaning and is rejected by name).\r\n  The CuTile runners now keep their own activation tuple, derived from the enforced\r\n  `_CUTILE_SUPPORTED_ACTIVATIONS` allowlist, so the CUTLASS-only PowLU does not leak into\r\n  their claimed support; every other backend rejects PowLU through its existing whitelist.\r\n- Docs: activation support matrix regenerated (`generate_moe_activation_matrix.py --check`\r\n  clean); per-expert override semantics documented in `flashinfer_moe_api.md`.\r\n\r\nPowLU is bounded (peak 4.02 at the default m=2.5 / 7.02 at m=3.5, decaying toward 1 as\r\ng grows), so a `limit` above the peak only clamps the linear branch.\r\n\r\n## \ud83d\udd0d Related Issues\r\n\r\nSame pattern as the SiTU-GLU addition in #4460 (adaptor + per-expert scalar slots) and the\r\nunified typed-activation plumbing in #4613.\r\n\r\n## \ud83d\ude80 Pull Request Checklist\r\n\r\nThank you for contributing to FlashInfer! Positive values mean FlashInfer is faster.\n|\nDS4 FlashInfer Humming GEMM speedup vs upstream Humming\nImplementation Notes\nThe Hopper FP4 compatibility path uses CUTLASS FP4 storage on SM90 and keeps\nCUDA native FP4 as a toolkit-dependent feature instead of a Hopper\nrequirement.\nThe mixed-input grouped GEMM scheduler now supports a precomputed work map.\nThis avoids doing grouped tile lookup and descriptor updates in the main GEMM\nhot path.\nThe folded weight-scale layout decouples model preprocessing from the\nselected\nTileK\n, which lets autotune choose among multiple\nTileK\nvalues\nwithout requiring multiple prepacked weight-scale layouts.\nThe pre-MMA E8M0 scale path fuses the weight scale into FP8 operands before\nWGMMA and applies the activation token scale in the epilogue.\nThe MXFP4xFP8 pre-MMA scale path is inspired by Humming's weight\npreprocessing/dequantization flow:\nhttps://github.com/inclusionAI/humming\nThe runtime FP8 activation quantization path computes per-token activation\nscales while expanding routed MoE rows.\nThe autotune validation path now rejects tactics that fail correctness before\ntiming and selection.\n\ud83d\ude80 Pull Request Checklist\nThank you for contributing to FlashInfer! "github:flashinfer-ai/flashinfer:6585", "source": "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/flashinfer-ai/flashinfer/pull/6585", "title": "feat(prims_ts): add delta input to dense FP8/NVFP4 "6bf5c2eb52eb267ecc12621f3bc99aab8c2f21a87749394fd323243feabb19bc", "body": "## \ud83d\udccc Description\n\nAdds an optional `delta` input to the PrimsTS dense FP8/NVFP4 GEMMs so callers can fuse an\nadditive per-element update \u2014 typically an unmerged LoRA term `scale \u00b7 (x\u00b7A\u1d40)\u00b7B\u1d40` \u2014 into the\nGEMM epilogue instead of running a separate add (and, for SwiGLU / QKV QK-norm+RoPE, instead\nof falling back to the unfused path).\n\n`delta` is a BF16 `[M, N]` tensor indexed by GEMM column. The epilogue adds it to the\ndequantized FP32 accumulator after weight/activation scales, bias and `qkv_scale`, and before\nthe SwiGLU activation, QK-norm/RoPE, or the output conversion:\n\n- `linear`: `out = bf16(acc_dequant + bias + delta)`\n- `swiglu`: the delta is added to both columns of each interleaved (value, gate) pair before `value * silu(gate)`\n- `qkv_qknorm_rope`: the delta is added before the RMS statistics and before the normalized store, so QK-norm sees the updated Q/K\n\nAPI:\n- New trailing `delta=None` on `fp8_linear`, `fp8_linear_swiglu`, `fp8_qkv_qknorm_rope`, `fp4_linear`, `fp4_linear_swiglu`, `fp4_qkv_qknorm_rope`, and on `PreparedFp4Linear.__call__`.\n- `PrimsTsGemmConfig.has_delta` (default `False`) is a compile-time flag; kernels compiled without it are unchanged. Delta kernels get a `_delta` name suffix and their own autotune cache entry.\n- A prepared NVFP4 projection builds the delta variant lazily on the first call that passes a `delta`, so the same object can be called with and without one.\n\nConstraints (validated in Python, raising `ValueError`): BF16 output only (FP8 output and packed NVFP4 output are rejected), `delta` must be a contiguous, 16-byte aligned `[M, N]` BF16 tensor on the GEMM's device, and `N % 8 == 0`.\n\nImplementation is in `GmemDResource` (`flashinfer/prims_ts/gemm/kernel.py`): one vector add in the main store path, scalar adds in the partial-N tail (gated and non-gated), and one in the QKV QK-norm statistics pass, all behind `const_expr(self.delta is not None)`.\n\n## \ud83d\udd0d Related Issues\n\n<!-- none -->\n\n## \ud83d\ude80 Pull Request Checklist\n\n### \u2705 Pre-commit Checks\n\n- [x] I have installed `pre-commit` by running `pip install pre-commit` (or used your preferred method).\n- [x] I have installed the hooks with `pre-commit install`.\n- [x] I have run the hooks manually with `pre-commit run --all-files` and fixed any reported issues.\n\n## \ud83e\uddea Tests\n\n- [x] Tests have been added or updated as needed.\n- [x] All tests are passing (`unittest`, etc.).\n\nNew tests in `tests/prims_ts/test_gemm_fp8.py` and `tests/prims_ts/test_gemm_fp4.py`:\n- `test_{fp8,fp4}_linear_delta_matches_sum`: result equals the same GEMM without delta plus `delta`; invalid deltas (wrong dtype/shape/alignment, FP8 output) raise. The FP4 variant also checks a prepared projection called without, with, and again without a delta.\n- `test_{fp8,fp4}_swiglu_delta_is_added_before_activation`: matches `linear(+delta)[:, 0::2] * silu(linear(+delta)[:, 1::2])`; packed NVFP4 output with a delta raises.\n- `test_{fp8,fp4}_qkv_delta_is_added_before_qknorm`: matches a QK-norm + RoPE reference applied to `linear(+delta)`.\n- `test_fp4_delta_tmem_overlap_matches_plain[linear|swiglu|qkv_qknorm_rope]`: the TMEM-overlap store path gives bit-identical results to the non-overlap path with a delta.\n\nRan on B200 (SM100): `tests/prims_ts/test_gemm_fp8.py`, `test_gemm_fp4.py`, `test_gemm_autotune.py` \u2014 43 passed, 65 skipped (SM103-only cases).\n\nCalls without `delta` compile the same kernels as before: every delta code path in the epilogue is behind a compile-time `const_expr` check on `has_delta`.\n\n## Reviewer Notes\n\n- **Compatibility:** existing calls are unaffected. The public changes are additive: a trailing `delta=None` on the six `fp8_*`/`fp4_*` functions and `PreparedFp4Linear.__call__`, and a trailing `has_delta: bool = False` field on `PrimsTsGemmConfig`. The internal `PrimsTsGemmRunner` input list grows from 16 to 17 entries (the benchmark is updated). Because the autotuner cache key includes the shape of every runner input, PrimsTS autotune results saved by earlier versions no longer match: with tuning enabled they are re-tuned once; without tuning the default tactic is used until they are.\n- **Why not reuse `bias`:** bias is a per-column `[N]` vector and is applied before `qkv_scale`/`scale` on the non-FP8 paths; the delta is per-element `[M, N]` and must come after all scaling.\n- **Rounding:** the delta is added in FP32 and rounded to BF16 once, so results can differ from \"BF16 GEMM, then BF16 add\" in the last bit; tests use a tolerance for the comparison against the no-delta GEMM and exact equality between kernel variants.\n- **Partial-N tail:** the scalar tail path adds the delta, but no shape in the test suite exercises N % 16 != 0 for these GEMMs, so that path has no dedicated test.\n- Low-rank fusion (passing `x\u00b7A\u1d40` and `B\u1d40` into the epilogue instead of a materialized `[M, N]` delta) is out of scope for this PR.\n\n<!-- This is an auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->\n\n## Summary by CodeRabbit\n\n* **New Features**\n  * FP8 and NVFP4 GEMM ope", "body_excerpted": true, "metadata_excerpt": "{\"number\": 6585, \"labels\": [], \"comments\": 1, \"state_reason\": null}", "metadata_excerpted": false}, {"id": "github:flashinfer-ai/flashinfer:5878", "source": "github", "kind": "issue", "url": "https://github.com/flashinfer-ai/flashinfer/issues/5878", "title": "bug(sampling): top_k_arr/min_p_arr are validated against input rows but indexed by output row, reading out of bounds when indices is longer than probs", "created_at": "2026-10-01T17:19:12Z", "updated_at": "2026-10-18T09:32:09Z", "fetched_at": "2026-10-20T12:14:40Z", "state": "open", "merged_at": null, "parent": null, "content_hash": "7024567381c8211f566f857f5d4d860d05423d8ce4cb14f71c1c8ef8b94a02da", "body": "### Summary\n\n`top_k_sampling_from_probs` and `min_p_sampling_from_probs` validate their per-request parameter tensor against the **input** batch but index it by the **output** row. When `indices` is longer than `probs.size(0)`, every output row at or past `probs.size(0)` reads past the end of the parameter tensor and silently samples with a garbage `top_k` / `min_p`.\n\n- `check_tensor_param(maybe_top_k_arr, probs)` \u2014 [`csrc/sampling.cu:216`](https://github.com/flashinfer-ai/flashinfer/blob/main/csrc/sampling.cu#L216) \u2014 requires `top_k_arr.size(0) == probs.size(0)`, via [`csrc/sampling_utils.h:34`](https://github.com/flashinfer-ai/flashinfer/blob/main/csrc/sampling_utils.h#L34). Same for `min_p` at [`csrc/sampling.cu:262`](https://github.com/flashinfer-ai/flashinfer/blob/main/csrc/sampling.cu#L262).\n- The kernels read [`top_k_arr[bx]`](https://github.com/flashinfer-ai/flashinfer/blob/main/include/flashinfer/sampling.cuh#L872) and [`min_p_arr[bx]`](https://github.com/flashinfer-ai/flashinfer/blob/main/include/flashinfer/sampling.cuh#L1129), where `bx` is `blockIdx.x`, i.e. the output row.\n\nThe sibling kernels get this right and index by the input row: [`top_p_arr[row_idx]`](https://github.com/flashinfer-ai/flashinfer/blob/main/include/flashinfer/sampling.cuh#L1006) in `TopPSamplingFromProbKernel`, and [`top_k_arr[row_idx]` / `top_p_arr[row_idx]`](https://github.com/flashinfer-ai/flashinfer/blob/main/include/flashinfer/sampling.cuh#L1228-L1229) in `TopKTopPSamplingFromProbKernel`. So only the two `[bx]` sites are wrong.\n\n### Reproduction\n\nOn a B200 (SM100), 4 input rows, 16 output rows via `indices = arange(16) % 4`, vocab 512. The parameter tensor is a length-4 view carved out of a 4096-element buffer whose tail holds a conspicuous value, so an out-of-bounds read returns that tail instead of faulting:\n\n| Call | Parameter tensor | Output rows violating the parameter |\n|---|---|---|\n| `top_k_sampling_from_probs` | length-4 `top_k`, all `1`, buffer tail `500` | **12 / 16** \u2014 exactly rows 4\u201315 |\n| `min_p_sampling_from_probs` | length-4 `min_p`, all `1.0`, buffer tail `0.0` | **12 / 16** \u2014 exactly rows 4\u201315 |\n| `top_p_sampling_from_probs` (control) | length-4 `top_p`, all `1e-6`, buffer tail `1.0` | **0 / 16** |\n\nWith `top_k = 1` every output row must return `argmax(probs[indices[i]])`; rows 4\u201315 do not. The `top_p` control isolates the cause to the `bx` / `row_idx` choice rather than anything about the harness.\n\nNote that `compute-sanitizer --tool memcheck` does **not** flag this: the read stays inside the PyTorch caching-allocator segment, so a memcheck-clean run is not evidence of safety here.\n\n<details>\n<summary>Repro script</summary>\n\n```python\nimport torch\nfrom flashinfer import sampling\n\ntorch.manual_seed(0)\nIN, OUT, V = 4, 16, 512\nprobs = torch.softmax(torch.randn(IN, V, device=\"cuda\"), dim=-1)\nindices = torch.arange(OUT, device=\"cuda\", dtype=torch.int32) % IN\n\n# Length-IN views carved out of a larger buffer whose tail holds a conspicuous value.\nk_buf = torch.full((4096,), 500, dtype=torch.int32, device=\"cuda\")\nk_buf[:IN] = 1\ntop_k = k_buf[:IN]\n\np_buf = torch.zeros(4096, dtype=torch.float32, device=\"cuda\")\np_buf[:IN] = 1.0\nmin_p = p_buf[:IN]\n\ntp_buf = torch.full((4096,), 1.0, dtype=torch.float32, device=\"cuda\")\ntp_buf[:IN] = 1e-6\ntop_p = tp_buf[:IN]\n\nargmax = probs.argmax(dim=-1)\n\n\ndef violations(out):\n    return [(i, int(out[i])) for i in range(OUT) if int(out[i]) != int(argmax[indices[i]])]\n\n\nout_k = sampling.top_k_sampling_from_probs(probs, top_k, indices=indices, deterministic=True)\nprint(\"top_k=1  violations:\", len(violations(out_k)), \"/\", OUT, violations(out_k)[:6])\n\nout_p = sampling.min_p_sampling_from_probs(probs, min_p, indices=indices, deterministic=True)\nprint(\"min_p=1  violations:\", len(violations(out_p)), \"/\", OUT, violations(out_p)[:6])\n\n# Control: top_p indexes by input row, so it must be unaffected.\nout_tp = sampling.top_p_sampling_from_probs(probs, top_p, indices=indices, deterministic=True)\nprint(\"top_p    violations:\", len(violations(out_tp)), \"/\", OUT)\n```\n\n```\ntop_k=1  violations: 12 / 16 [(4, 189), (5, 226), (6, 216), (7, 345), (8, 459), (9, 92)]\nmin_p=1  violations: 12 / 16 [(4, 320), (5, 147), (6, 350), (7, 300), (8, 42), (9, 113)]\ntop_p    violations: 0 / 16\n```\n\n</details>\n\n### Fix\n\nTwo options, and the choice is a contract decision rather than a mechanical one:\n\n1. Index by the input row \u2014 change `top_k_arr[bx]` to `top_k_arr[row_idx]` and `min_p_arr[bx]` to `min_p_arr[row_idx]`, matching the existing validation and the two sibling kernels.\n2. Keep output-row indexing and fix the validation to use the output batch instead of `probs`, which is what `top_k`/`min_p` being *per-request* arguably implies.\n\nOption 2 matches what the docstrings suggest (`indices` maps outputs to distributions, so a per-request parameter is naturally per-output) and is the convention #5745 uses for `seed`/`offset`, which validates against `output.size(0)`. experts with top-8 routing, hybrid 512-token sliding window plus NoPE full attention, 1M context, Harmony chat format). Beam support hasn't shipped in any vLLM release or nightly yet, so the recipe is Docker-only on the `vllm/vllm-openai:reflection-beam` image.\n\n### What the builder renders\n\n| Hardware | Default command | Status |\n|---|---|---|\n| B300 (page default) | NVFP4, TP4, `--attention-backend FLASHINFER --moe-backend flashinfer_trtllm` | \u2705 verified, release checkpoint |\n| B200 / GB200 / GB300 | NVFP4, TP4 (same flags) | eligible, not run |\n| H200 | NVFP4 via Marlin, TP8. **Recommended: the FP8 variant, TP8** | \u2705 verified (FP8 and NVFP4), proto checkpoints |\n| H100 | NVFP4 via Marlin, TP8 | 80 GB budget simulated on H200 |\n\n- Variants: `default` = NVFP4 (`reflection/Beam-1.0-501B-A23B-NVFP4`), `fp8` (`-FP8`), `bf16` (base repo, only on 8x B200/B300).\n- The site has no per-GPU default variant, so H200 also opens on NVFP4. The guide tells H200 users to switch to FP8, the same approach as vllm-project/recipes#1012.\n- Features: the `beam` reasoning and tool-call parsers are on by default. DFlash2 (`reflection/Beam-1.0-501B-A23B-DFlash2`, `num_speculative_tokens: 7`) is opt-in.\n- Strategies: single-node TP / TEP / DEP.\n- Install: Docker only, `vllm/vllm-openai:reflection-beam`. No pip path: the nightly wheels don't carry the Beam implementation.\n- Hardware: NVIDIA only. The four AMD profiles are marked `unsupported` (CUDA-only kernels, CUDA-only image).\n- `date_added`: 2026-10-16, the launch day.\n\n### Evidence\n\nEach verified run passed: `/health`, `/v1/models`, chat with `finish_reason: stop`, `reasoning_content`, parsed `tool_calls`.\n\nThe numbers below stay out of the recipe, following the repo convention: few recipes publish measured throughput, and upstream held back such a PR as premature (#58). Workload: 8 chat prompts (code, math, prose, translation) \u00d7 8 runs, up to 1,500 output tokens, temperature 0.6, reasoning on. Every command is the one the site generates. Each run passed a 12-check suite:\n- health, chat, and reasoning-effort levels\n- single, parallel, streamed, `required` and round-trip tool calls\n- a ~24K-token prompt\n- prefix-cache reuse\n- 16 concurrent requests\n\nKV-offload runs also passed a reload check: clear the GPU cache, resend the ~32K-token prompt, and the hit must come from the offload tier with the same answer at temperature 0.\n\n| Area | Configs | Result |\n|---|---|---|\n| NVFP4 | TP4, TEP8, DEP8, each \u00b1 DFlash2 | \u2705 |\n| NVFP4, Rust frontend | TP4 \u00b1 DFlash2 | \u2705 |\n| FP8 | TP8, TEP8, DEP8, TP8 + DFlash2 | \u2705 |\n| BF16 | TP8, TEP8, DEP8, TP8 + DFlash2 | \u2705 (140 GiB of weights per GPU) |\n| 1M context | 1,034,106-token prompt, needles at 2% / 50% / 97% | \u2705 all recalled |\n| KV offload | Simple, Offloading CPU, Offloading CPU+FS (also reloads from disk after a restart), LMCache | \u2705 |\n| KV offload | Mooncake (both topologies) | \u26d4 not tested: this pod caps memlock at 8 MB, so RDMA can't register memory |\n\nNotes:\n- **Tool calling needs the fix.** On builds without it, the Rust frontend returns 500 and Python silently drops the grammar.\n- **Site issues found during the runs.**\n  - The Mooncake install line pulls the CUDA 12 wheel; `mooncake-transfer-engine-cuda13` is needed here.\n  - The Offloading CPU default of 512 GiB needs that much `/dev/shm`. It passed at 32 GiB.\n- **Startup.** It took 160\u2013820 s with caches warm, and no run hit the engine-ready timeout.\n\n### Before merge\n\n- [ ] Confirm the parameter counts: the recipe says 501B / 23B, the early card said 502B / ~24B.\n- [ ] When a vLLM release carries Beam support and the tool-calling fix, restore the pip install path and re-run the B300 default on it.\n- [ ] Re-run H200 FP8 on the release checkpoint (the badge currently rests on proto checkpoints).\n- [ ] Mooncake on a pod with `--ulimit memlock=-1`.\n- [x] ~~BF16, Rust frontend, KV offload (Simple / Offloading / LMCache), TEP / DEP on B300~~ (see the matrix above)\n- [ ] Not run: GPUs other than B300/H200, FP8 KV cache, multi-node.\n\n\ud83e\udd16 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\n\n\n\n\n\n\n", "body_excerpted": false, "metadata_excerpt": "{\"number\": 1132, \"labels\": [], \"comments\": 1, \"state_reason\": null}", "metadata_excerpted": false}, {"id": "github:vllm-project/recipes:1135", "source": "github", "kind": "pr", "url": "https://github.com/vllm-project/recipes/pull/1135", "title": "Muse Spark Open: align NVIDIA path with the vLLM launch blog", "created_at": "2026-10-16T19:33:37Z", "updated_at": "2026-10-16T19:40:23Z", "fetched_at": "2026-10-20T12:14:43Z", "state": "merged", "merged_at": "2026-10-16T19:40:20Z", "parent": null, "content_hash": "991287c68b784402bcf97c7e2507f42a2f8e85a52dece18cdf9a4c04de406770", "body": "Aligns the Muse Spark Open recipe with the NVIDIA deployment in the [launch blog](https://vllm.ai/blog/2026-10-15-muse-spark-open).\n\n- `default_strategy: single_node_tep`: the builder previously fell back to plain TP. On Hopper the indexed GEMM is much faster for this model. This PR makes `get_humming_moe_gemm_type()` return `indexed` when all of these hold:\n\n- the experts are MXFP4 with DeepSeek V4 routing;\n- `model_type` is `deepseek_v41` or `deepseek_v41_text`;\n- the device is CUDA SM90;\n- there are no all2all kernels, or the backend is `allgather_reducescatter`.\n\nWithout EP, Humming already picks indexed, so nothing changes there. `VLLM_HUMMING_MOE_GEMM_TYPE` still overrides the choice. Since #56997, Humming is the default MXFP4 MoE backend on SM90, so V4.1 with EP on Hopper otherwise runs the grouped GEMM by default; this PR moves that default path to indexed too. The rule applies with BF16 activations and with per-token FP8 activations (`--quantization-config.moe.activation fp8_per_token`).\n\nThe MXFP4 MoE method (DeepSeek V4/V4.1, Kimi-K3, plain mxfp4) now runs `quantization_config.moe.activation`:\n\n```bash\nvllm serve deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash -tp 8 --enable-expert-parallel \\\n    --moe-backend humming --quantization-config.moe.activation fp8_per_token\n```\n\n- Humming takes the named format as a fixed input schema, with its input-schema fallback off.\n- Other explicit backends must run the named format, and `auto` keeps only the backends that do. Before, these models ignored the name on every backend. Now a backend that does not run it stops vLLM, on every platform (CUDA, ROCm, XPU, CPU). The DeepSeek V4 MegaMoE backends (`deep_gemm_mega_moe`, `aiter_mega_moe`) build their own experts and do not read the name. On ROCm, the Kimi-K3 AITER path, which skips the backend selector, now refuses a named activation instead of ignoring it. For example, `fp8_per_token` with `flashinfer_trtllm` now stops vLLM instead of running MXFP8. A draft model with its own `speculative_config.moe_backend` inherits the target's `quantization_config`, so the same rule applies to it.\n- The startup log prints the format Humming runs: `Humming MoE activations: fp8_per_token (group size 0, scale dtype float32)`. The line appears for every Humming MoE model, gpt-oss included.\n- On SM90 Humming, `mxfp8` and `fp8_per_block_dynamic` stop vLLM at startup, before the weights load. Humming has no e8m0 input scales on WGMMA, and humming 0.1.16 rejects fp32 group scales with MXFP4 weights (#60600). Group-wise scales are also 1.2x to 24x slower than per-token in the SM90 Humming GEMM at M >= 256 (numbers in the review reply).\n- `VLLM_HUMMING_INPUT_QUANT_CONFIG` still works as on main, with no early check: a format Humming cannot run fails while the weights convert, as before. With `--moe-backend humming`, setting it together with `moe.activation` is an error.\n- gpt-oss is unchanged. With no name, nothing changes.\n\n## Test Plan\n\n```bash\npytest tests/kernels/moe/test_moe.py -k \"named_activation or activation_on_sm90 or humming or indexed\"\npytest tests/quantization/test_quantization_config_args.py \\\n    tests/quantization/test_humming_mxfp4_block_fp8.py tests/kernels/moe/test_b12x.py \\\n    tests/kernels/moe/test_topk_softplus_sqrt.py tests/quantization/test_gfx950_moe.py\npytest tests/models/kimi_k3/test_gfx942_int4.py\npytest tests/kernels/moe/test_ocp_mx_moe.py -k unsupported_reasons\n```\n\n`test_humming_deepseek_v41_hopper_uses_indexed_gemm_with_ep` calls `get_humming_moe_gemm_type` and checks that V4.1 with EP gets indexed, with and without `allgather_reducescatter`, and that DeepEP, SM100 and DeepSeek V4 keep the grouped GEMM. It does not need the humming package.\n\n`test_deepseek_v4_mxfp4_backend_must_run_named_activation` checks on any GPU that `auto` and `flashinfer_trtllm` refuse `fp8_per_token`, and that `humming` refuses the env JSON plus a name (\"not both\"). `test_humming_deepseek_v4_activation_on_sm90` checks that SM90 Humming accepts `fp8_per_token` and refuses `mxfp8`. It skips on other GPUs, so in CI it runs only in the optional H100 FP8 MoE kernels job; `kernels-moe-test` runs on L4. The Kimi-K3 AITER refusal has no unit test: it is a short guard, and a test would have to mock the whole K3 path.\n\n`user_moe_activation_override()` (was `_user_moe_activation_override()`) is now public because `quantization/mxfp4.py` imports it; the existing monkeypatches in `test_b12x.py`, `test_ocp_mx_moe.py` and `test_gfx950_moe.py` use the new name.\n\nStartup check: `vllm serve deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash -tp 8 --enable-expert-parallel --moe-backend humming` on 8x H200, with these additions:\n\n- A: `--quantization-config.moe.activation fp8_per_token` and DSpark "https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51217", "title": "[MoE] Generalize masked activation for padded layouts", "created_at": null, "updated_at": "2026-09-01T15:34:16Z", "fetched_at": "2026-10-19T23:09:13Z", "state": "merged", "merged_at": "2026-09-01T15:34:14Z", "parent": null, "content_hash": "3ff047c7c4adfa0852aa74bf9b7db886ab2d5d965ce0c000fcc81c040feeab3d", "body": "## Summary\n\n- add one shared count-aware MoE activation entry point for a flat valid prefix (`[T, D]`) and per-expert valid prefixes (`[E, T, D]`)\n- support the gated and non-gated activation set explicitly, with a fail-closed `NotImplementedError` when a future activation lacks a masked implementation\n- route Humming, batched Triton, and batched Marlin through `ApplyMoEActivationConfig` instead of backend-specific SITU/clamped-SiLU branches\n- select Humming GEMM layout automatically: `indexed` without EP, `grouped_contiguous` with EP, and `grouped_masked` for batched experts; environment overrides remain available but are validated choices\n- pass DeepEP v2's cudagraph valid-prefix metadata into Humming so activation skips padded receive rows\n- validate padded/invalid expert IDs before indexing `expert_map` in the Humming permute path\n\n## Why\n\nDeepEP v1 and v2 expose different padded layouts. Legacy/batched execution has one valid prefix per expert, while DeepEP v2 has one valid prefix in a flat cudagraph receive buffer. Backend-specific activation branches had diverged and made SITU especially expensive in Kimi K3.\n\nThe shared op makes the layout contract explicit and specializes the flat versus batched branch at compile time. The Kimi investigation also found a correctness issue before activation: DeepEP v2 uses `-1` for padding, but Humming's permute preprocessing read `expert_map[topk_id]` before checking the ID. Invalid IDs now become the expert sentinel before any map access. The regression covers both `-1` and a positive out-of-range ID.\n\n## Model validation\n\nThe final branch state was retested after the latest merge from `main`. No `VLLM_HUMMING_MOE_GEMM_TYPE` override was set in these runs.\n\n### DeepSeek-V4 Flash\n\nTested `deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731` in eager mode with FP8 KV cache:\n\n- DP=1, no DeepEP: Humming selected `indexed`; the server reached healthy state and a deterministic arithmetic request returned the correct answer (42)\n- DP=2 / EP=2 with `--all2all-backend deepep_v2`: vLLM used NCCL 2.30.7, selected `DeepEPV2All2AllManager` and Humming `grouped_contiguous`, reached healthy state, and returned the same correct answer\n- earlier GSM8K 100 evaluation: DP=1 scored **0.96 accuracy / 0 invalid**; DP=2 / EP=2 scored **0.95 accuracy / 0 invalid**\n\nThe close DP1/DP2 evaluation scores and the final post-merge serving smoke test provide the model-level control for the DeepEP v2 layout. The earlier standalone DSV4 topology test was removed when this PR was refocused on the shared DP/EP activation contract, so it is not claimed as current CI coverage.\n\n### Kimi K3\n\nServed `mgoin/Kimi-K3-pruned75` on four B300 GPUs with:\n\n```bash\nchg run -g=4 -- env EP_DISABLE_GIN=1 vllm serve mgoin/Kimi-K3-pruned75 \\\n  --tokenizer-mode kimi_k3 --trust-remote-code --dtype bfloat16 \\\n  --language-model-only \\\n  --data-parallel-size 4 --enable-expert-parallel \\\n  --all2all-backend deepep_v2 --moe-backend humming \\\n  --max-model-len 8192 --max-num-batched-tokens 512 \\\n  --max-num-seqs 16\n```\n\n- DP=4 / EP=4 used NCCL 2.30.7 and selected `DeepEPV2All2AllManager` and Humming `grouped_contiguous`\n- the default non-eager server captured exactly **7 PIECEWISE + 5 FULL** breakable CUDA graphs; this model's V2 runner reports compilation mode `NONE`, so this validates CUDA graphs rather than TorchInductor\n- the server reached healthy state and deterministic arithmetic requests returned coherent, correct output containing 42, with none of the prior repeated-`@` corruption\n\nBefore the invalid-ID guard, the same run emitted repeated `@` tokens; GSM8K 100 was 0% accurate with 100% invalid responses and every request exhausted 256 tokens. After the guard, chat-mode GSM8K 100 was **15% accuracy / 33% invalid** with 18,611 output tokens. Inspection showed the remaining invalid responses were mostly coherent but unfinished reasoning from this 75%-pruned checkpoint; a 10-question diagnostic at 1,024 tokens improved to 30% / 20% invalid. The latter small sample is not treated as a model-quality result. The relevant correctness signal is the disappearance of repeated-token corruption together with the focused native regressions.\n\nKimi required the 512-token batch cap on four B300s. A 2,048-token cap OOMed because DeepEP v2's worst-case receive/permute scratch must cover `max_tokens * dp_size * topk` rows.\n\n### Runtime and shutdown notes\n\nThe final DeepEP v2 tests used the shared development `.venv` with `nvidia-nccl-cu13==2.30.7` and `EP_DISABLE_GIN=1`. The runtime reported NCCL 2.30.7 and DeepEP v2 available. NCCL 2.29.7 is too old for the DeepEP v2 GIN API; vLLM requires NCCL >= 2.30.4 for this backend.\n\nStopping the multi-rank model servers with the default `--shutdown-timeout 0` force-killed peer processes and DeepEP subsequently reported `cudaErrorIllegalAddress` while destroying an elastic buffer. This happened after successful health checks and generation requests and was not observed on the inference path. so\nnelement()\n= M_global x top_k, while each rank's GEMM only touches ~1/ep_size of those rows.\nThe value only drives kernel config selection: the Python block-size ladder in\n_prepare_indexed_gemm_kwargs\nand the humming launcher's variant lookup (\nfind_kernel_launch_data\nin launcher.cpp). Correctness is unaffected, the launcher validates against real tensor shapes. So under EP every humming MoE GEMM picks its config for a workload ~4x larger than what the rank actually computes (EP4).\nWe tried the obvious fix, a graph-safe host estimate\nmax(1, M * top_k / ep_size)\n(exact counting needs a D2H sync, illegal under CUDA graph capture). It moves every working point to the true local shape and passes unit tests, but serving regressed:\narm\ndecode tok/s/req\nvs parent\nparent (current code)\n52.2\n-\nEP-aware estimate\n49.5\n-5.1% (perm p=0.003, n=10/arm)\nEP-aware + ladder bands recalibrated from per-point kernel microbench\n-\n-4.0% e2e (C48 median, 3-arm check)\nSetup: DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash (384 routed experts, top_k 6, MXFP4 experts), TP4/EP4, 4x H200-class GPUs, DSPark speculative decoding, random workload at 32-48 concurrency, unique-seed A/B protocol with flush_cache between rounds, cross-session reproducible.\nPer-cubin enumeration against the deployed heuristic ladder explains why. The ladder bands were calibrated against the global-element scale, and the two gemm types disagree about what the input even means:\nindexed, routed rows ~750-2100: the local-rows input picks kernels 22-26% faster than the global-scale input\ngrouped, same points: opposite direction, the global-scale input wins by 8-12%\nsmall decode CUDA-graph tiers (<200 rows): both land in the same band, zero effect\nre-mapping ladder bands empirically flipped kernel rankings once real routing skew and CUDA-graph replay entered the picture\nA single scalar cannot carry both total routed rows and per-expert density once num_local_experts != num_experts. For reference, the masked/DeepEP-LL path already uses\nexpected_m * num_local_experts\n, and DeepEP-normal dispatch carries exact host-side\nnum_recv_tokens_per_expert\n.\nReproduction\nAny EP>1 run with\n--moe-runner-backend humming\n(default gemm type indexed). The wrong value is observable without serving, it is just\ntopk_ids.nelement()\nunder EP:\n# decode bs=48, top_k=6, EP4: topk_ids (48, 6).nelement() = 288 passed as\n# valid_shape_m while the rank's GEMM only handles ~72 rows.\n# See estimate_local_valid_shape_m in\n# python/sglang/srt/layers/moe/moe_runner/humming.py\nServing A/B protocol:\npython3 -m sglang.benchmark.serving --dataset-name random --random-input-len 1024 --random-output-len 256 --random-range-ratio 1 --num-prompts 384 --max-concurrency 48\n, unique seed per round, /flush_cache between rounds, drop first warmup round, median of 4.\nEnvironment\nPython: 3.12.3, PyTorch: 2.13.0+cu130, CUDA: 13.0 (driver 580.105.08)\nGPU 0-3: NVIDIA L20X (SM90)\nsglang: 0.0.0.dev1+g20518d851, sglang-kernel: 0.4.7\nflashinfer: 0.6.18, "2026-10-20T08:03:40Z", "fetched_at": "2026-10-20T12:18:55Z", "state": "open", "merged_at": null, "parent": null, "content_hash": "6e911757ceb8e2740289808f6317dfca7ec2cab3cbfaa2373a8ae3577248deca", "body": "## Motivation\r\n\r\nThis PR adds an SGLang-native CUTLASS **MXFP4A8 fused MoE backend for\r\nSM90/Hopper**, targeting MXFP4-expert models such as DeepSeek-V4-Flash.\r\n\r\nThe implementation keeps expert weights in MXFP4 (E2M1 with block-32 E8M0\r\nscales), dynamically quantizes activations to FP8 E4M3, and executes both\r\nexpert GEMMs on Hopper FP8 tensor cores.\r\n\r\nThe current scope is deliberately limited to **TP deployments with EP=1**.\r\n`EP>1` is not supported by this PR and is rejected explicitly during weight\r\ninitialization.\r\n\r\n## Summary\r\n\r\n- Add an MXFP4-to-FP8 mixed-input CUTLASS path for SM90.\r\n- Add load-time Humming-compatible MXFP4 weight preprocessing.\r\n- Add an EP1 fused runtime covering routing metadata preparation, input\r\n  permutation and quantization, GEMM1, SwiGLU, requantization, GEMM2, output\r\n  permutation, router weighting, and top-k reduction.\r\n- Add shape-specialized SM90 schedulers for serving-time token buckets.\r\n- Preserve the existing INT4A8 path unchanged.\r\n- Add numerical tests for MXFP4 conversion, scale layouts, grouped GEMM, and\r\n  cross-backend consistency against FlashInfer.\r\n\r\nThe backend is selected with:\r\n\r\n```bash\r\n--moe-runner-backend cutlass\r\n```\r\n\r\n### Execution flow\r\n\r\n```mermaid\r\nsequenceDiagram\r\n    participant X as BF16 input\r\n    participant R as Top-k routing\r\n    participant S as SGLang fused path\r\n    participant F as FlashInfer\r\n    participant W as MXFP4 weights\r\n    participant Y as MoE output\r\n\r\n    X->>R: tokens and topk=6\r\n    W-->>S: Humming-compatible view\r\n    W-->>F: Humming view\r\n    par SGLang EP1 path\r\n        R->>S: GPU-built routing metadata\r\n        S->>S: reorder + per-token FP8 quant\r\n        S->>S: grouped GEMM1\r\n        S->>S: SwiGLU + FP8 requant\r\n        S->>S: grouped GEMM2\r\n        S-->>Y: reorder + router-weight reduction\r\n    and FlashInfer\r\n        R->>F: topk IDs + router weights\r\n        F->>F: bucketed tactic lookup\r\n        F->>F: fused MoE body\r\n        F-->>Y: fused finalize\r\n    end\r\n    Note over S,F: Same logical MXFP4 weights and routing inputs<br/>Different runtime orchestration and scheduling\r\n```\r\n\r\n## Implementation\r\n\r\n### MXFP4A8 tensor-core path\r\n\r\nWeights remain in checkpoint-native MXFP4. Activations are quantized per token\r\nto FP8 E4M3. The E2M1 values\r\n`{0, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 6}` are exactly representable in E4M3, so the\r\nE2M1-to-E4M3 conversion uses a lossless signed-nibble lookup.\r\n\r\nThe mixed-input mainloop derives the scale group from\r\n`TileK / PackedScalesNum`, allowing the existing INT4A8 group-128 path and the\r\nnew MXFP4A8 group-32 path to share the same mainloop without changing INT4\r\nbehavior.\r\n\r\n### Load-time weight preprocessing\r\n\r\nThe EP1 path prepares one runtime representation after checkpoint loading:\r\n\r\n- interleaved MXFP4 weights;\r\n- folded E8M0 offsets;\r\n- one residual scale per expert.\r\n\r\nThe representation follows the SM90 Humming preprocessing semantics. Runtime\r\nexecution is implemented by SGLang AOT kernels.\r\n\r\nOnly the fused representation is retained for EP1 to avoid storing two complete\r\nweight layouts on H20.\r\n\r\n### Fused EP1 execution\r\n\r\n`cutlass_mxfp4a8_fused_moe_core` performs:\r\n\r\n1. expert-offset, problem-size, and permutation preparation;\r\n2. routed-input reorder and per-token FP8 quantization;\r\n3. grouped GEMM1;\r\n4. fused SwiGLU, optional clamp, and FP8 requantization;\r\n5. grouped GEMM2;\r\n6. output reorder, FP32 router-weight multiplication,\r\n   `routed_scaling_factor`, and top-k reduction.\r\n\r\nFor `M <= 64`, metadata preparation and shuffled input quantization use a\r\nbounded fused CUDA path. Larger batches use the AOT metadata and quantization\r\nkernels without host synchronization. Intermediate tensors are retained in\r\nreusable workspaces.\r\n\r\n### SM90 scheduling\r\n\r\nThe implementation includes:\r\n\r\n- a persistent single-warpgroup ping-pong kernel for tiny and arbitrary\r\n  serving-time shapes;\r\n- a precomputed grouped scheduler with prebuilt TMA descriptors for validated\r\n  large-M buckets;\r\n- a packed warp-shuffle GEMM2 epilogue.\r\n\r\n| Tokens | GEMM1 config | GEMM2 config |\r\n|---:|---:|---:|\r\n| `M <= 64` | 100 | 100 |\r\n| `M = 2048` | 313 | 313 |\r\n| `M = 4096` | 320 | 334 |\r\n| `M >= 8192` | 322 | 334 |\r\n| Other shapes | 101 | 101 |\r\n\r\n## Validation\r\n\r\n### DeepSeek-V4-Flash end-to-end A/B\r\n\r\nHardware and runtime:\r\n\r\n- 8x NVIDIA H20;\r\n- native DeepSeek-V4-Flash checkpoint;\r\n- TP8 / EP1;\r\n- FP8 KV cache and DSV4 attention;\r\n- separate fresh containers for the two backends;\r\n- identical package versions except for the locally built `sglang-kernel`\r\n  wheel;\r\n- FlashInfer 0.6.18 W4A8 as the upstream baseline.\r\n\r\nServing workload:\r\n\r\n- exact paired token-ID prompts;\r\n- 128 requests;\r\n- concurrency 32;\r\n- output length 1536;\r\n- repeat 1.\r\n\r\n| Input -> output | CUTLASS output tok/s | FlashInfer output tok/s | Throughput | Mean TTFT |\r\n|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|\r\n| 8192 -> 1536 | 1399.19 | 1247.18 | **+12.19%** | **-17.26%** |\r\n| 16384 -> 1536 | 933.52 | 814.49 | **+14.61%** | **-16.95%** |\r\n| 32768", "body_excerpted": true, "metadata_excerpt": "{\"number\": 34682, \"labels\": [{\"id\": 7486299040, \"node_id\": \"LA_kwDOLCAjRs8AAAABvjfboA\", \"url\": FlashInfer reuses its internal expert permutation to map those scales to routed rows, including global-to-local expert mapping under EP.\nThe original\n#3738\nABI briefly shipped before being reverted from FlashInfer 0.6.17 by\n#4411\n. It required downstream runtimes to construct routed-row residual tensors during every forward. SGLang does not support that older ABI.\nThe new option therefore requires both:\nflashinfer-python >= 0.6.18\npreprocess_moe_weights_for_sm90_mixed_gemm_humming\nSGLang currently pins FlashInfer 0.6.17. This PR does not change that global pin. With 0.6.17, selecting\nfp8\nfails immediately with an actionable error, while all existing FlashInfer paths remain compatible. The dependency, cubin, and JIT-cache pins should be upgraded together in a follow-up change.\nGPU validation for this PR used FlashInfer 0.6.18 built from commit\n8044d94bf9acc5369857baf88d28906bb32bf264\n, which contains the corrected API from FlashInfer\n#4431\n.\nModifications\nRuntime selection and compatibility\nAdd\nfp8\nto\n--flashinfer-mxfp4-moe-precision\n.\nSelect the new path only for\nflashinfer_mxfp4\non SM90.\nKeep W4A16 as the default SM90 behavior.\nReject the SM90-specific\nfp8\noption on SM100 and SM120 to avoid confusing it with their existing MXFP8 activation paths.\nRequire the corrected FlashInfer 0.6.18+ API instead of accepting the older routed-row residual ABI.\nPass\nuse_wfp4afp8_humming=True\nonly when the new path is selected. GPT-OSS MXFP4 quantization path and the DeepSeek-V4 MXFP4 expert path.\nKernel invocation\nConstruct the five Humming quant-scale slots expected by the corrected FlashInfer API:\nFC1 folded weight "body": "**End-to-end validation and issue reporting:** Our shape coverage and correctness have not yet been validated end to end in model serving. If you encounter (1) end-to-end accuracy degradation, (2) unsuitable API requirements, such as unnecessarily requiring contiguous inputs, or (3) faster kernel benchmarks but worse end-to-end performance because the shapes optimized by the kernel do not cover the actual model-serving workload, please open an issue and tag @yyihuang with the affected kernel, the missing or problematic shape(s), and relevant model/serving details or a reproducer. We will resolve reported issues within **6 hours**.\n\n## Motivation\n\nTrack integration of Cake kernels already delivered through FlashInfer into SGLang, grouped by the models that requested them. The upstream inventory is [flashinfer-ai/flashinfer#4254](https://github.com/flashinfer-ai/flashinfer/issues/4254); model requirements below come from its subissues and the linked implementation PRs.\n\nThe proposed integration is through FlashInfer's public/prepared APIs. Each task should connect the relevant model operator, weight/cache layout, and execution mode to an eligible kernel, then measure the complete serving path. Upstream kernel benchmarks are useful starting points, not claims of SGLang end-to-end speedup.\n\n**Snapshot: 2026-10-03 (UTC; October 2 in US Pacific); FlashInfer main [46340689a5ab](https://github.com/flashinfer-ai/flashinfer/commit/46340689a5ab5665ab39f6281dac4d021d7ae94b).** \u201cMerged\u201d below means available in upstream FlashInfer, not necessarily in a released wheel or integrated here. Open follow-ups are explicitly marked and are not included in the merged coverage. Most listed kernels target SM100/SM103; exact architecture, SM-count, dtype, layout, and shape admission remain API-specific. Let the serving adapter retain its existing backend for configurations outside those contracts.\n\nNotation: `T/M` = token rows, `B` = requests, `Q` = query tokens per request, `S` = KV length. MoE tuples are **(hidden size, expert intermediate size, number of experts, top-k)**; an intermediate size marked \u201clocal\u201d is after TP sharding. Enumerated benchmark shapes are evidence points, not an assertion that every combination is supported.\n\n## Integration and acceptance\n\n- [ ] Pin a FlashInfer revision/release containing the required merged APIs and register backend selection at the existing operator boundary.\n- [ ] Check the selected release branch separately: [#5978](https://github.com/flashinfer-ai/flashinfer/pull/5978) is merged into `release-v0.7.1` only and reverts the BF16 head-dim-256 small-M speculative-decode route because of an intermittent B200 hang. This is a separate route from the E4M3/page-64 DCP entries below; a merge on the release branch does not establish a fix on main.\n- [ ] Prepare weights, plans, output buffers, and workspaces before graph capture; account for changing request lengths, routing, state indices, and graph buckets.\n- [ ] Validate each listed model/checkpoint, quantization recipe, TP/EP/CP configuration, and state/cache layout. Cover prefill, decode, speculative verification, ragged batches, and prefix-cache reuse where relevant.\n- [ ] Compare against the current engine backend on model quality, TTFT, TPOT, throughput, and memory.
